# IMPLEMENTATION LOG — V3.9 Flight-Delay Data Collection

> **Integrated operator edition — operator-manual strengthened and run-report synchronized 2026-09-03 UTC / 2026-09-02 PDT.** This is the same current safety-re-audited Implementation Log, reorganized and updated rather than scientifically redesigned. The binding scientific/experimental authority remains `V3.9_DataCollectPlan_V3.9-f.8_FINAL_REAUDITED_EXECUTION_SAFE_2026-09-02.md` §§0–21. This Log tells the human operator and repository-capable AI how to implement, verify, execute, stop, and record that Plan. It cannot override the Plan.

## How to use this Log

**Read every working session:** §0 current truth → §1 implementation runbook/blueprint → §2 Replit shell commands → §3 code/scripts → §4 phase roadmap → §5 offline verification → §6 gate board → §7 GO/NO-GO/handoff.

**Engineering reference when implementing/debugging:** §§8–26.

**Foundational/teaching/reference that you normally do not reread every day:** §§27–31.

**Project records:** §§32–35. **Only §36 is archive/superseded history.**

The reorganization changes navigation, not V3.9 methodology or authorization. Any repository path or command is current only after repository evidence verifies it.

### Current section order (no legacy numbering in the active manual)

| Priority | Current sections | Use |
|---|---|---|
| **Daily / operator** | §§0–7 | Current truth, implementation steps, Replit commands, code/scripts, phases/gates, offline verification, gate status, stop/return decisions |

| **Project records** | §§32–35 | Issues, workstreams, dated change history, maintenance rules |
| **Archive only** | §36 | Superseded commands, historical reports/status snapshots, and original old-log material; never current execution authority |

## 0. Authority, evidence boundary, current readiness, and status vocabulary

1. `V3.9_DataCollectPlan_V3.9-f.8_FINAL_REAUDITED_EXECUTION_SAFE_2026-09-02.md` §§0–21 is the sole scientific/operational authority.
2. This Log is the implementation/runbook authority derived from that Plan. It tells the human operator and repository-capable AI what to implement, what to run, what evidence closes a step, and when to stop. It cannot weaken or override the Plan.
3. `V3.9_RUN_REPORTS_AND_EVIDENCE.md` is the evidence ledger. It records what actually happened. A report may contain a **PROPOSED_NEXT_STEP**, but that proposal is advisory only and must identify the controlling Log §1/§2 step; a report cannot create a new requirement, command, gate, PASS criterion, authorization, or scientific rule.
4. Active §§0–35 are current Log authority. §36 is archive/history only. Historical commands/statuses never become current merely because they are preserved.
5. If Plan and Log disagree, create `DOC_CONFLICT`, stop the affected path, and correct the Log to the Plan. If a Run Report and the Log disagree about what happens next, preserve the report as evidence and follow the current Log until the discrepancy is adjudicated.
6. A file existing is not implementation. A component advances only through:

```text
NOT_INSPECTED
  → DOCUMENTED
  → CODED_STANDALONE
  → UNIT_TESTED
  → PRODUCTION_WIRED
  → OFFLINE_INTEGRATION_TESTED
  → LIVE_VERIFIED       (only where live evidence is required)
```

7. `PRODUCTION_WIRED` requires the real production caller, schema/migration, configuration owner, failure path, and mapped tests. A helper, script, migration file, or passing pure-function test alone cannot receive that status.
8. Historical or earlier evidence never becomes a current PASS by copy/paste. Re-run or explicitly re-adjudicate it against the current commit/config/schema/provider scope.

### 0.4 Canonical status vocabulary

| Status | Exact meaning |
|---|---|
| `DOC_ALIGNED` | Plan and Log express the same current rule; no code claim |
| `REPO_REQUIRED` | The rule is documented, but repository evidence is required before implementation status can advance |
| `NOT_INSPECTED` | Repository evidence has not been examined |
| `NOT_RUN` | A required test or gate procedure has not been executed on the current implementation |
| `PARTIAL_EVIDENCE` | Some mapped cases passed, but the requirement/test/gate is not closed |
| `CODED_STANDALONE` | Code exists but production caller/wiring is not proven |
| `UNIT_TESTED` | Focused deterministic unit test passed on recorded commit |
| `PRODUCTION_WIRED` | Real production caller and schema/config path proven |
| `OFFLINE_INTEGRATION_TESTED` | Production path passed required offline/integration cases |
| `LIVE_BLOCKED` | Live/provider evidence is required but sequence/prerequisites are unmet |
| `LIVE_VERIFIED` | Current live evidence ID, timestamp, account/channel, commit/config hash, and result are recorded |
| `INSUFFICIENT_SAMPLE` | No rule failed, but pre-frozen evidence minimum was not met; never PASS |
| `FAIL` | A pass criterion failed; affected path stops |
| `MISMATCH` | Billing/accounting/manifest/protocol identity disagrees; paid work pauses |
| `DOC_CONFLICT` | Plan/Log disagree; affected path stops until corrected |
| `BLOCKED_HUMAN` | Specific owner authorization/legal/retention decision is missing |
| `BLOCKED_NOT_AUTHORIZED` | A paid or mutating action lacks an exact, unexpired human authorization record |
| `BLOCKED_OFFLINE_AND_LIVE_EVIDENCE` | Both repository closure and current external/account evidence are missing |
| `BLOCKED_REFERENCE` | A required external frame/reference artifact is missing or not licensed/verified |
| `RUNTIME_NOT_VERIFIED` | The required runtime/configuration state was not observable in this audit |

Status advancement is monotonic only for the same commit, schema, config, provider pin, and evidence scope. A relevant change invalidates dependent PASS states and returns them to the appropriate earlier state.

## 1. Master implementation runbook — start-to-finish timeline for you and the AI agent

**Purpose of this section:** this is the main implementation manual. Read it from top to bottom when deciding what to build next. It converts the binding Plan into a concrete sequence of code work, schema work, tests, gates, operator actions, evidence, and stop points. §2 gives the exact shell-command timeline for the same sequence.

**Authority rule:** this section explains *how to implement*. It never changes the Plan. If a later reference section is more detailed, that detail supports this sequence; it does not create a second competing sequence.

### 1.1 Current checkpoint — where the supplied Run Reports actually leave us

The project is **still in Phase 0 / offline closure**, despite useful progress and some out-of-order read-only Gate-0 observations.

| Phase-0 checkpoint | Evidence in supplied report | Current status | What must happen next |
|---|---|---|---|
| Repository intake | 42 disruption modules inventoried; branch `main` reported | `PARTIAL_PASS` | Re-record current HEAD/worktree after the latest edits. |
| Safe default | `ADB_AUTO_COLLECT=false` reported | `PASS_REPORTED` | Reverify every session and before any live command. |
| 104-rule mapping / reverse map | No complete zero-missing evidence supplied | `BLOCKED` | Generate current requirement→owner→test→evidence and reverse maps. |
| Migrations 0024/0025 | Registered in migration runner | `PARTIAL_EVIDENCE` | Fresh DB + upgrade + repeat boot + constraints/indexes + rollback/recovery must pass. |
| Command surface | `v39:*` commands reported wired; several mappings listed | `PARTIAL_EVIDENCE` | Verify every closure command is real/non-stub and invokes the correct production owner. |
| Unit/project tests | 208 tests / 13 files reported PASS | `PASS_FOR_DISCOVERED_SUITE` | Expand/repair cases so TEST-001–028 are all closed; existing suite count is not the closure criterion. |
| TEST-001–028 | Multiple IDs still NOT_RUN/PARTIAL | `BLOCKED` | Implement offline fixtures/failure-injection and production owners until all required cases PASS. |
| Typecheck | 57 full-repo errors reported | `FAIL` | Fix/adjudicate to exit 0; “0 in `_v3.ts`” is not closure. |
| Lint | no complete evidence | `NOT_EVIDENCED` | Run configured lint; if not configured, record `NOT_CONFIGURED` as a blocker until explicitly adjudicated. |
| Production build | no complete evidence | `NOT_EVIDENCED` | Run production build and record exit 0/artifact. |
| Scanner | `CURRENT_CONTRADICTIONS=0` reported | `PASS_REPORTED` | Re-run on final Phase-0 commit. |
| Calendar contract | 26/3/2 shape fixture + SAT reported | `PARTIAL_EVIDENCE` | Keep as TEST-017 evidence; final calendar waits until Phase 5 measured/frozen inputs. |
| Gate 0 | balance/no-subs/feed reads observed | `PROVENANCE_ONLY / BLOCKED` | Do not continue Gate 0 until Phase-0 closure is reviewed. |

**Immediate rule:** do not ask “what gate comes next?” yet. The current question is “which Phase-0 closure item is still non-PASS?” Work the list below until all Phase-0 items are closed, then stop and return with evidence.

### 1.2 Human operator vs repository-capable AI — exact responsibilities

**You / human operator**

1. Keep the Plan and this Log as the contract; do not manually rewrite scientific rules during implementation.
2. Open Replit / the authoritative runtime, provide repository access, and run/paste §2 commands when needed.
3. Never paste API keys/secrets into this Log or the Run Reports.
4. Approve exact AUTH records only when §1/§2 reaches a live/mutating step and the previous prerequisite is PASS.
5. On any `FAIL`, `MISMATCH`, unknown prerequisite, unresolved settlement, or unexpected provider/config change: stop and preserve evidence.
6. Bring the Run Reports back at the mandatory handoffs in §1.12.

**Repository-capable AI/coding agent**

1. Inspect the real repository before making a status claim.
2. Preserve user work; record branch/HEAD/worktree before and after edits.
3. Map every binding requirement to actual production code/schema/config/tests/evidence before calling it implemented.
4. Reuse one production implementation; scripts/CLI wrappers must call production services rather than reimplement rules independently.
5. Implement missing R1–R7/S1–S5 and the expanded f.8 requirements in dependency order below.
6. Add both happy-path and refusal/failure-injection tests.
7. Run the §2 verification loop after each work package and the full Phase-0 closure sequence at the end.
8. Write evidence into the Run Reports. It may propose a next step only as `PROPOSED_NEXT_STEP` tied to a specific §1/§2 step.
9. It may **not** self-authorize provider/account/paid actions, invent a new probe/credit amount, skip a gate, or reinterpret a partial result as PASS.

### 1.3 Phase 0 — detailed implementation timeline (DO THIS NOW)

Phase 0 is not “write some files and run unit tests.” It is complete only when the production paths, schema, config, tests, traceability, and aggregate preflight all close on one recorded commit/config/schema state.

#### 1.3.1 Step 0A — re-establish current repository truth after the reported edits

**Goal:** make sure the evidence you are about to build belongs to the code that actually exists now.

AI/operator actions:

1. Record repository root, remote (redacted), branch, current HEAD, worktree status, runtime, package manager, lockfile, database/migration runner, and production startup path.
2. Verify `ADB_AUTO_COLLECT=false` in configuration **and** runtime/default path.
3. Inventory current `v39:*` commands and mark each `REAL`, `STUB`, `MISSING`, or `WRONG_OWNER`.
4. Record a new `REPO-INTAKE-YYYYMMDD-###` because later runs changed code after the original intake.

**PASS:** current HEAD/worktree/toolchain/entry points are known and no automatic collection can start.

#### 1.3.2 Step 0B — build the requirement map before more implementation edits

Create/regenerate the authoritative mapping:

`Plan requirement / REQ ID → R/S or expanded f.8 work package → production owner → schema/config owner → TEST ID(s) → evidence artifact → status`.

Also regenerate the reverse map:

`production file/function/table/script → requirement IDs → test IDs → evidence`.

Use §12 (104 requirements), §13 (reverse map), §14 (file map), §15 (owner rule), and §16 (88-component verification map). Any unmapped binding requirement remains a Phase-0 blocker.

These are the Plan §15 runtime/code deltas. “Reported file exists” is not enough; prove the production caller and tests.

#### 1.3.6 Step 0F — prove migrations, not merely registration

For every current binding migration (including reported 0024/0025 or renamed successors):

#### 1.3.7 Step 0G — scripts/CLI wrappers come after production owners

For each canonical `v39:*` command:

2. reject wrappers that duplicate scientific/accounting logic;

4. add fail-closed prerequisite/authorization checks;
5. prove exit codes and evidence IDs;
6. remove/replace any stub command.

The current Run Report provides evidence that several commands were wired; §2.1 records what is evidenced and what must still be reverified.

#### 1.3.8 Step 0H — complete TEST-001–028 as offline closure tests

**Critical clarification:** TEST-001–028 are behavior requirements. They are **offline fixtures/failure-injection by default**. A test is not excused merely because the real behavior later receives live evidence at a gate.

Examples:

- FIDS boundary/role/range/retry logic can be exercised against contract-pinned fixtures/mocked provider responses offline; live Gate 1/0.5 later validates real behavior.
- raw-before-2xx and migration behavior require a proper test DB, not the production DB.
- canary/settlement/cap logic uses deterministic fake balance/SEND/failure sequences offline; Gate 3/4 later adds live evidence.
- Terms/security lifecycle uses fixture content classes/expiry stores offline; actual subscribed Terms/owner approval comes at prerequisite P.

Every TEST ID must end in one of: `PASS` or an explicitly Plan-permitted later-live subcheck with the **offline portion already PASS**. A blanket `NOT_RUN because live` does not close Phase 0.

#### 1.3.9 Step 0I — full quality/reproducibility closure

Run and record:

- complete discovered project tests;
- TEST-001–028 mapped cases;
- full-repository typecheck **exit 0**;
- lint exit 0 (or explicit `NOT_CONFIGURED` blocker until adjudicated);
- production build exit 0;
- migration test matrix;
- registry and traceability zero-missing counts;
- dictionary/lineage completeness;
- scanner pass;
- aggregate preflight pass.

The reported “57 errors, 0 in V3.9” is a useful diagnostic baseline but remains a Phase-0 blocker under the current Log.

#### 1.3.10 Step 0J — write Phase-0 closure report and STOP

The report must include current HEAD/worktree/schema/config hashes, exact commands/exit codes, TEST-001–028 statuses, full-suite count, migration evidence, typecheck/lint/build, registry/traceability/scanner/preflight, unresolved warnings, and artifact hashes.

Then **STOP. Do not continue Gate 0.** Bring the evidence back for review.

### 1.4 Gate 0 — account/budget identity (ONLY AFTER Phase-0 review authorizes continuation)

Purpose: verify the already-implemented accounting/refusal logic against the real account, not to redesign it.

Required evidence:

1. active 60,000-unit plan entitlement unchanged;
2. `subscription_channel`;
3. billing-cycle start/end, used/remaining REST/API units;
4. current Alert-credit balance and refill history;
5. refill conversion/caps and balance caps;
6. pinned provider endpoint/tier/unit-cost evidence;
7. separate Alert-credit and seven-category REST budget trees;
8. protected floor/unallocated/run-total invariants;
9. exact Gate-0 artifact hash.

**Existing report note:** the supplied report already observed balance 2,900/no active subscriptions/feed availability. Keep that as provenance. Because Phase 0 was not closed and the formal authorization/evidence contract was incomplete, it does not make Gate 0 PASS and does not authorize continuation.

### 1.5 PREPAID_SECURITY_RETENTION prerequisite P

After Gate 0 PASS, verify the actual channel/Terms and all content classes before Gate 1 stores new provider/reference content:

- Terms/Plan retention rights and human/legal approval where required;
- licensed traffic/region reference use;
- credentials/secret redaction;
- webhook TLS/auth/replay controls;
- raw-before-2xx behavior;
- primary/replica/backup/log retention and deletion propagation;
- incident stop;
- shared settlement settings.

Unknown affected content class/control = `BLOCKED`.

### 1.6 Gate 1 → reference freeze → frame build → pre-probe freeze

Only after prerequisite P PASS:

1. **Gate 1:** acquire current provider-backed coverage with exact command/commit/provider version/cost/hash evidence.
2. **Reference freeze:** freeze licensed traffic source, metric, period, cut/tie/missing rules, region mapping/overrides, and normalization inputs.
3. **Frame build:** rebuild/hash final frame; report explicit `UNCLASSIFIED`/`UNMAPPED` and feed eligibility.
4. **Pre-probe freeze:** from the final frame freeze exactly 12 dual-eligible HUB-tier candidates including WSSS/OMAA, ordered replacements, yield reference/fallback rules, scoring/normalization/protocol, Stage-1/2 rules, budget-day semantics, and source/frame hashes.

No smoke/probe is allowed before all four artifacts are complete.

### 1.7 Authorized safety smoke → Gate 2 Stage 1/Stage 2

1. Freeze exact smoke AUTH scope/ceiling/expiry/stop owner.
2. Run one tiny production-path smoke.
3. Require raw-before-2xx, owned subscription lifecycle, >0 usable item when required, ≥3 stable authoritative balance reads, exact external/internal reconciliation, zero delivery failures, cleanup/inactivity, no foreign billable subscription.
4. **Return with smoke evidence before Stage 1.**
5. Run Stage 1 sequentially over frozen candidates/replacements with cumulative 500-credit `probe_budget_day_id` cap behavior.
6. After every candidate: close subscription → settle → reconcile → classify PASS/FAIL/INSUFFICIENT_SAMPLE/censored → write evidence before moving on.
7. Produce ranking; stop for review if ranking/INSUFFICIENT_SAMPLE/replacement decisions are material.
8. Run Stage 2 only on valid promoted candidates under the frozen protocol/authorization.
9. Lock final five only after all valid evidence settles and rank-invariance checks pass.
10. **Return after Gate 2 before Gate 3.**

### 1.8 Gate 3 → Gate 0.5 → Gate 4 → Gate 5

#### Gate 3 — official canary

Same production path, exact R1/R3 semantics, `tol=0`, shared stable-settlement rule, raw/item accounting, failures=0, cleanup. Return before Gate 0.5.

#### Gate 0.5 — dedicated adequately sampled pilot

One frozen airport/window and pre-frozen minimums. Validate provider clocks, movement semantics/actuality, selected T, primary target, FIDS boundaries/roles, cadence/trajectory, recovery latency. `INSUFFICIENT_SAMPLE` does not freeze measured values. Return before Gate 4.

#### Gate 4 — cap/reliability

Offline scaled state-machine proof must already exist; then run only the small authorized live reconciliation/second-start check. Never spend toward 1,900 just to test the cap. Return before Gate 5.

#### Gate 5 — population observability

Validate independent FIDS population against snapshot/capture/outcome funnels for frozen intervals. Webhook capture is a parallel dimension. Return before readiness/FREEZE.

### 1.9 Phase 5 — readiness and FREEZE

After all gates PASS:

1. reverify history/weather/retention readiness;
2. solve the **final** calendar using measured/frozen inputs; the earlier SAT fixture is only TEST-017 evidence;
3. freeze exact Alert and REST/API budget trees;
4. freeze split-assignment rule/hash;
5. complete manifest/registry/reference/frame/gate/provider/account/code/schema hashes;
6. run scanner/preflight again;
7. write `FREEZE` evidence.

**STOP and return with the final manifest/hash. FREEZE itself does not start Phase 6.**

### 1.10 Phase 6 — authorized 31-batch-day collection

Only a separate exact AUTH tied to the frozen manifest/hash may transition `ADB_AUTO_COLLECT=false → true`.

Runtime rules:

- execute solver-generated 31-batch-day calendar (`26×4h + 3×2×2h + 2×up-to-6h`), with actual calendar dates allowed to span >31 days for washout;
- enforce 1,900 Alert-credit ceiling per immutable Phase-6 budget day plus frozen margin/reservations;
- keep Alert vs REST units separate;
- preserve exact batch/subscription ownership;
- settlement/reconciliation/failure mismatch pauses collection;
- any relevant provider/config/manifest drift pauses and may require re-verification;
- `v39:phase6:pause` must be immediately available.

Do not modify the frozen methodology silently during the run.

### 1.11 Phase 7 — evaluation

Use the PRE/AIRBORNE snapshot/evaluation paths already implemented and tested before collection. Materialize actual rows, apply the frozen split rule, hash-protect test rows before tuning, run the Month-1 pilot model/evaluation suite, information-per-credit diagnostics, collection-mechanism ablation, and Model POST pilot. Do not build first-time core ETL after seeing the collected data.

### 1.12 Mandatory handoff schedule — when you come back with reports

1. **NOW / Phase 0:** finish §1.3 and §2.2–§2.7 → write Phase-0 closure report → STOP → return **before any further Gate-0/provider/account progression**.
2. **After Gate 0 + prerequisite P:** return if anything is partial/unknown/changed; otherwise do not skip the required artifact sequence.
3. **After safety smoke:** mandatory return before Stage 1.
4. **During Stage 1:** automatic sequential continuation is allowed only when the current candidate is fully closed/settled and the frozen authorization/protocol remains valid. Return immediately on FAIL, material `INSUFFICIENT_SAMPLE`, cap mismatch, provider change, cleanup failure, or authorization expiry.
5. **After Stage-1 ranking:** return before Stage 2 if promotion/replacement/rank-invariance needs review.
6. **After Gate 2:** return before Gate 3.
7. **After each Gate 3, Gate 0.5, Gate 4, Gate 5:** return before the next gate.
8. **After FREEZE:** mandatory return before Phase 6 authorization.
9. **During Phase 6:** return immediately on pause/failure/mismatch; otherwise use the frozen daily evidence cadence rather than asking the model to redesign the experiment mid-run.

There is no arbitrary “return within X minutes” rule. The continuity requirement is state/protocol-based: close/settle/reconcile the current stage and remain inside its frozen timing/authorization conditions before the next stage.

## 2. Replit / shell-command runbook — exact operator timeline tied to §1

**Purpose:** §1 tells you *what must be implemented*. §2 tells you *what you type in Replit, when you type it, what PASS means, and what happens after FAIL*. This is the only current shell-command authority. Archived commands in §36 are history only.

### 2.1 Command status from the supplied Run Reports

Do not assume every `v39:*` name is complete merely because the command surface exists. The supplied report explicitly evidences these mappings/results:

| Interface / artifact | Supplied evidence | Current operator interpretation |
|---|---|---|
| `v39:scanner` / `v39:preflight` → `scripts/v39_preflight_consistency.ts` | reported wired and scanner returns `CURRENT_CONTRADICTIONS=0` | scanner mapping is useful; aggregate Phase-0 preflight is **not** PASS while TEST/typecheck/lint/build/etc. remain open |
| `v39:gate0:inspect` → Gate-0 evidence script | reported working read-only evidence | preserve results as provenance; do not continue Gate 0 until Phase-0 review |
| `v39:gate1:coverage` → `scripts/measure_coverage.ts` | reported mapping | later command only; prerequisite P + AUTH required |
| `v39:frame:build` → `scripts/build_stratified_catalog.ts` | reported mapping | later offline artifact command after Gate-1/reference freeze |
| `v39:probe:stage1/2` → `scripts/anchor_probe.ts` | reported mapping | **not authorization**; must satisfy current AUTH/preprobe/settlement/refusal contract before use |
| `v39:gate3:canary` → `scripts/credit_canary.ts` | reported mapping | **not authorization**; current production semantics/tests must be proven first |
| `v39:calendar:solve` → `scripts/calendar_solve.ts` | reported added and 26/3/2 fixture SAT | use now only for TEST-017 fixtures; final Phase-5 solve happens later |
| `v39:test:full` / Vitest | 208 tests reported PASS | useful suite evidence, not TEST-001–028 closure |
| `v39:typecheck` | 57 full-repo errors reported | currently FAIL for closure |
| `v39:lint`, `v39:build`, `v39:migrate:check`, `v39:registry:check`, `v39:traceability:check` | no complete PASS evidence supplied | verify/map/run during Phase 0 |

**Before using any canonical command:** `npm run` must show a real mapping. If it is missing, a stub (`echo ...`), or calls the wrong owner, STOP and have the coding agent map it correctly before the operator proceeds.

### 2.2 Start every Replit implementation session with this read-only safety block

Run from the repository root:

```bash
set -euo pipefail
export ADB_AUTO_COLLECT=false

printf 'repo=%s
' "$(pwd)"
printf 'utc=%s
' "$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)"
git branch --show-current
git rev-parse HEAD
git status --short
git remote -v | sed -E 's#(https?://)[^/@]+@#<redacted>@#g'

node --version
npm --version
npm run

# Confirm the v39 surface and detect accidental auto-collection/provider-risk defaults.
npm run 2>&1 | grep -E 'v39:' || true
grep -RIn --exclude-dir=node_modules --exclude-dir=.git   -E 'ADB_AUTO_COLLECT|AUTO_COLLECT|maxDeliveryRetries|phase6|57[,.]?900|1[,.]?900' . || true
```

**Operator decision:**

- unexpected branch/worktree/HEAD relative to the evidence report → STOP and let the AI reconcile before edits;
- `ADB_AUTO_COLLECT` true/default-on anywhere in the preparation path → P0 FAIL, fix before continuing;
- missing/stub closure command → AI maps the command first;
- otherwise proceed to the current Phase-0 work package in §1.3.

### 2.3 The implementation micro-cycle after every code/schema/R/S work package

Use this pattern after each coherent change; do not wait until the very end to discover breakage.

```bash
# 1) Show exactly what changed.
git status --short
git diff --check
git diff --stat

# 2) Run the targeted test file(s) for the work package.
# Replace the examples below with the mapped current test file(s); do not invent a PASS if a required case is absent.
npx vitest run <target-test-file-1> <target-test-file-2>

# 3) Run the complete discovered test suite so shared behavior does not regress.
npm run v39:test:full

# 4) Re-run typecheck/build checks appropriate to the change.
npm run v39:typecheck
```

If full typecheck is still failing, the AI must record the exact errors and continue only within Phase 0 to fix them. A baseline comparison may diagnose where errors came from, but **cannot turn a nonzero exit into PASS**.

#### 2.3.1 Suggested targeted test-file map from the supplied repository report

These are reported test filenames and are useful for the micro-cycle. They do not replace §5 TEST-ID coverage.

| Work package | Targeted Replit command examples |
|---|---|
| FIDS + timestamp contract | `npx vitest run tests/provider_fids.test.ts tests/timestamps_raw.test.ts` |
| Raw ingress + extractor + immutable events | `npx vitest run tests/raw_ingress.test.ts tests/flight_extractor.test.ts tests/dedup_events.test.ts` |
| Flight identity/codeshares/retimes | `npx vitest run tests/flight_identity.test.ts` |
| History/weather/as-known features | `npx vitest run tests/weather_history.test.ts` |
| PRE/AIRBORNE cutoff/trajectory logic | `npx vitest run tests/airborne_pipeline.test.ts` plus any new dedicated snapshot tests added for TEST-011/012 |
| `m_i` / zero-yield / coverage floor | `npx vitest run tests/adaptation.test.ts` |
| Calendar/crossover | `npx vitest run tests/calendar_solver.test.ts` |
| Budget/cap/accounting | `npx vitest run tests/budget_accounting.test.ts tests/gates.test.ts` |
| Manifest/scanner/registry guards | `npx vitest run tests/scanner_manifest.test.ts` plus new registry/traceability tests |

If a TEST-ID behavior has no corresponding test case/file yet, **create the offline fixture/failure-injection test**; do not label it “live only” and skip it.

### 2.4 Special command rules after migrations/schema changes

Immediately after any migration/schema change:

```bash
export ADB_AUTO_COLLECT=false
npm run v39:migrate:check
npm run v39:test:offline
npm run v39:test:full
npm run v39:typecheck
```

`v39:migrate:check` must exercise a disposable/test database and prove fresh/upgrade/repeat/constraints/rollback-recovery. If no test DB is available, status is `BLOCKED_TEST_DB`; do not use the production DB as a casual substitute and do not call TEST-024 PASS.

### 2.5 Special command rules after command/script wrapper changes

After editing `package.json`, a `scripts/*.ts` wrapper, or a `v39:*` mapping:

```bash
npm run

grep -n '"v39:' package.json || true

# Run the command only in its offline/dry-run mode at this stage.
# If the command has no safe offline/dry-run/refusal path, add one before it is considered operator-ready.
```

For any future external/paid command, prove **before live use** that missing/expired AUTH, wrong airport/window, excessive ceiling, foreign subscription, unresolved predecessor, or `ADB_AUTO_COLLECT` mismatch returns nonzero before SEND/mutation.

### 2.6 Phase-0 closure sequence — the exact block you run only after the AI believes the code work is complete

```bash
set -euo pipefail
export ADB_AUTO_COLLECT=false

npm run v39:repo-intake
npm run v39:migrate:check
npm run v39:test:offline
npm run v39:test:full
npm run v39:typecheck
npm run v39:lint
npm run v39:build
npm run v39:registry:check
npm run v39:traceability:check
npm run v39:scanner
npm run v39:preflight
```

**PASS interpretation:**

| Command | Must establish before you advance |
|---|---|
| `v39:repo-intake` | current branch/HEAD/worktree/runtime/schema/entry-point evidence |
| `v39:migrate:check` | fresh/upgrade/repeat/constraints/recovery PASS |
| `v39:test:offline` | TEST-001–028 required offline portions PASS; no blanket live-only skips |
| `v39:test:full` | complete discovered project suite exit 0 |
| `v39:typecheck` | **full repository exit 0** |
| `v39:lint` | exit 0; if absent, explicit blocker/adjudication instead of fake PASS |
| `v39:build` | production build exit 0 and artifact evidence |
| `v39:registry:check` | no missing/unowned binding config |
| `v39:traceability:check` | 104-rule forward/reverse unmapped counts exactly zero |
| `v39:scanner` | no current Plan/Log/repo stale-rule contradiction |
| `v39:preflight` | aggregates all of the above; it cannot be an alias for scanner-only success |

The supplied report's earlier `v39:preflight` scanner PASS **does not** satisfy this aggregate definition while typecheck/tests/migration/lint/build/traceability remain incomplete.

### 2.7 What to do when a Phase-0 command fails

Use this decision table instead of improvising new commands:

| Failure | Immediate action | Next allowed command |
|---|---|---|
| targeted/unit test FAIL | fix the mapped production owner or test fixture; record issue | rerun the same targeted test, then full suite |
| TEST-ID missing | implement the required offline case; map it in §5/§12 | targeted test → `v39:test:offline` |
| migration check FAIL / no test DB | create/fix disposable DB fixture/migration path; do not touch live casually | rerun `v39:migrate:check` |
| typecheck nonzero | fix all errors or record unresolved blocker; no readiness exception | rerun `v39:typecheck` |
| lint/build nonzero | fix; no later gate | rerun failed command |
| registry/traceability missing count >0 | map owners/tests/config/evidence | rerun corresponding check |
| scanner contradiction | create `DOC_CONFLICT`/issue and reconcile to Plan | rerun scanner after correction |
| preflight nonzero | read exact failing prerequisite; return to its owner | only the failing prerequisite command |
| accidental provider call during offline step | STOP, record incident/cost/evidence, keep paid work blocked | no later live command until reviewed |

### 2.8 Mandatory Phase-0 STOP / report handoff

When and only when every §2.6 command passes on the same current evidence scope:

1. write/update the Run Report with exact commands, exit codes, current HEAD, test counts, TEST-001–028 matrix, migration artifacts, typecheck/lint/build, hashes, warnings, and unresolved issues;
2. set `PROPOSED_NEXT_STEP = Log §1.4 Gate 0 review` (advisory only);
3. **STOP in Replit and bring the report back.**

Do not run another Gate-0/account/provider command yet. The earlier read-only Gate-0 observations remain in the report and will be adjudicated at the review.

### 2.9 After review authorizes Gate 0 — exact operator sequence

These are later commands, **not for the current Phase-0 session**.

First verify the command's resolved plan and exact AUTH/evidence IDs. Then:

```bash
export ADB_AUTO_COLLECT=false
npm run v39:gate0:inspect -- --auth AUTH-YYYYMMDD-G0 --evidence-id GATE-0-YYYYMMDD-001
```

Gate-0 PASS requires the full §1.4 evidence set. A balance read alone is not PASS.

Then prerequisite P:

```bash
npm run v39:security:verify -- --evidence-id SECURITY-YYYYMMDD-001
```

Default behavior must be offline/local. If any sub-check contacts provider/account or mutates external state, it must refuse unless an exact AUTH covers that sub-check.

### 2.10 Gate 1 → reference/frame/pre-probe artifact commands

Only after Gate 0 + prerequisite P PASS:

```bash
npm run v39:gate1:coverage -- --auth AUTH-YYYYMMDD-G1 --evidence-id GATE-1-YYYYMMDD-001
npm run v39:reference:freeze -- --evidence-id REFERENCE-YYYYMMDD-001
npm run v39:frame:build -- --evidence-id FRAME-YYYYMMDD-001
npm run v39:preprobe:freeze -- --evidence-id PREPROBE-YYYYMMDD-001
```

Stop on any missing hash/source/license/reference/frame/shortlist/protocol item. Do not jump directly from coverage to a probe.

### 2.11 Safety smoke and Gate 2 commands

Only after the pre-probe freeze and exact AUTH:

```bash
npm run v39:smoke:safety -- --auth AUTH-YYYYMMDD-SMOKE --evidence-id RUN-YYYYMMDD-SMOKE
```

**Mandatory STOP after smoke settlement/report before Stage 1.**

Later Stage 1/2 invocations use the frozen runner. The exact candidate/window must come from the pre-probe artifact, not a human ad-hoc ICAO:

```bash
npm run v39:probe:stage1 -- --auth AUTH-YYYYMMDD-P1 --evidence-id RUN-YYYYMMDD-P1
npm run v39:probe:stage2 -- --auth AUTH-YYYYMMDD-P2 --evidence-id RUN-YYYYMMDD-P2
```

The runner must print the resolved candidate/window/cap/protocol before SEND and refuse a mismatch. After each candidate, the runner owns cleanup/settlement/reconciliation/evidence before continuing.

### 2.12 Gate 3 / Gate 0.5 / Gate 4 / Gate 5 commands

Each requires its predecessor PASS and a new exact authorization where external/mutating:

```bash
npm run v39:gate3:canary -- --auth AUTH-YYYYMMDD-G3 --evidence-id GATE-3-YYYYMMDD-001
npm run v39:gate05:pilot -- --auth AUTH-YYYYMMDD-G05 --evidence-id GATE-05-YYYYMMDD-001
npm run v39:gate4:live-check -- --auth AUTH-YYYYMMDD-G4 --evidence-id GATE-4-YYYYMMDD-001
npm run v39:gate5:population -- --auth AUTH-YYYYMMDD-G5 --evidence-id GATE-5-YYYYMMDD-001
```

**Return after every gate before the next.** On `FAIL`, `MISMATCH`, unresolved settlement, or material `INSUFFICIENT_SAMPLE`, do not ask the AI for a creative workaround; preserve evidence and return to the controlling §1 step.

### 2.13 Phase-5 final readiness / FREEZE commands

Only after Gate 5 PASS and all required measured/frozen inputs exist:

```bash
export ADB_AUTO_COLLECT=false
npm run v39:calendar:solve
npm run v39:registry:check
npm run v39:traceability:check
npm run v39:scanner
npm run v39:preflight
npm run v39:freeze -- --evidence-id FREEZE-YYYYMMDD-001
```

The reported earlier SAT calendar is a solver-contract fixture; this invocation creates the final evidence only when its inputs are the frozen Gate outputs.

**STOP and return with manifest/hash before Phase 6.**

### 2.14 Phase-6 commands — never run without separate final authorization

```bash
npm run v39:phase6:start -- --auth AUTH-YYYYMMDD-FINAL --manifest-sha256 '<exact frozen hash>'
npm run v39:phase6:status
npm run v39:phase6:pause
```

`phase6:start` must refuse unless every prior evidence identity/hash and the exact authorization match. `phase6:pause` must remain immediately available.

### 2.15 Run Report “next steps” rule — how the other AI may help without contradicting the Log

It is **good** for the implementation AI to tell you what it thinks should happen next, because that makes the evidence file usable. But use this format:

```text
CURRENT_EVIDENCE_STATE: <facts only, with evidence IDs>
CONTROLLING_LOG_STEP: §1.x / §2.x
PROPOSED_NEXT_STEP: <one next action derived from that section>
WHY: <which prerequisite is PASS/BLOCKED>
COMMAND_TO_RUN: <only if §2 already authorizes it for the current state>
STOP_CONDITION: <what result makes us stop/return>
AUTH_REQUIRED: yes/no; exact AUTH ID if yes
```

Rules:

1. `PROPOSED_NEXT_STEP` is advisory, not authority.
2. The report never creates a new probe, airport, budget, threshold, gate, formula, or command sequence.
3. If the report's proposal conflicts with the Plan/Log, preserve the report and follow the Plan/Log.
4. A report may suggest **fix/debug commands** after a failure only if they are offline/diagnostic and do not change the frozen methodology or bypass prerequisites. Any new live/paid/mutating command requires the normal §1/§2 sequence and AUTH.
5. After a code fix, the AI reruns the failed test plus the required regression/closure commands; it does not simply mark the issue fixed in prose.
6. Long raw outputs remain in the Run Reports/evidence artifacts, not duplicated into this Log.
